# MOMENTUM FOR THE FUTURE

Annual Report  
**2024**

<!-- PAGE 2 -->

## ABOUT SUNTEC REIT

Listed on 9 December 2004 on the Main Board of the Singapore Exchange Securities Trading Limited ("SGX-ST"), Suntec Real Estate Investment Trust ("Suntec REIT") is one of the leading real estate investment trusts in Singapore, owning income-producing real estate that is primarily used for office and/or retail purposes. As at 31 December 2024, Suntec REIT has assets under management of over S\$12 billion with properties in Singapore and key Australian cities of Sydney, Melbourne and Adelaide as well as in London, United Kingdom.

In Singapore, Suntec REIT's portfolio comprises office and retail properties in Suntec City, 66.3% interest in Suntec Singapore Convention & Exhibition Centre ("Suntec Singapore"), one-third interest in One Raffles Quay ("ORQ"), one-third interest in Marina Bay Financial Centre Towers 1 and 2 and the Marina Bay Link Mall ("MBLM" and collectively known as the "MBFC Properties"). The properties in Australia include 177 Pacific Highway and 21 Harris Street in Sydney, 50.0% interest in Southgate Complex and 50.0% interest in Olderfleet, 477 Collins Street in Melbourne and 55 Currie Street in Adelaide. In United Kingdom, Suntec REIT owns a 50.0% interest in Nova Properties and The Minster Building in London.

Suntec REIT is managed by an external manager, ESR Trust Management (Suntec) Limited (formerly known as ARA Trust Management (Suntec) Limited). The Manager is focused on delivering regular and stable distributions to Suntec REIT's unitholders, and to achieve long-term growth in the net asset value per unit of Suntec REIT, so as to provide unitholders with a competitive rate of return on their investment.

## ABOUT ESR TRUST MANAGEMENT (SUNTEC) LIMITED

Suntec REIT is managed by ESR Trust Management (Suntec) Limited, a wholly-owned subsidiary of ESR Asset Management Limited (formerly known as ARA Asset Management Limited).

ESR Asset Management Limited is part of the ESR Group ("ESR"), Asia-Pacific's leading New Economy real asset manager and one of the largest listed real estate investment managers globally. ESR's fully integrated fund management and development platform extends across Australia and New Zealand, Japan, South Korea, Greater China, Southeast Asia, and India, including a presence in Europe. ESR provides investors with a diverse range of real asset investment and development solutions across private and public investment vehicles. ESR's focus on New Economy real assets offers customers modern solutions for logistics, data centres, and infrastructure and renewables. ESR's purpose, Space and Investment Solutions for a Sustainable Future, drives ESR to manage sustainably and impactfully for the communities where it operates and the spaces it develops to thrive for generations to come. ESR Group Limited is listed on The Stock Exchange of Hong Kong (HKSE: 1821.HK). Visit [www.esr.com](http://www.esr.com) for more information.

The Manager is responsible for the management and administration of Suntec REIT, as well as the implementation of Suntec REIT's strategic long-term growth.

## Reviewing Year 2024

### JAN

- Achieved distributable income from operations of \$106.3 million for the half year from 1 July 2023 to 31 December 2023. Distribution per unit ("DPU") for the half year amounted to 3.659 cents.

### FEB

- Entered into a \$950 million sustainability-linked loan facility.

### MAR

- Appointment of Mr Shen Jinchu, Jeffrey and Mr Yap Chee Meng as members of the Nominating and Remuneration Committee.

### APR

- Unitholders approved all resolutions tabled at Suntec REIT's annual general meeting held on 18 April 2024.
- Achieved distributable income from operations of \$44.0 million for the quarter from 1 January 2024 to 31 March 2024. DPU to unitholders for the quarter amounted to 1.511 cents.

### JUL

- Achieved distributable income from operations of \$88.7 million for the half year from 1 January 2024 to 30 June 2024. DPU to unitholders for the half year amounted to 3.042 cents (including DPU of 1.511 cents for the quarter from 1 January 2024 to 31 March 2024).

### OCT

- Suntec REIT retained theGRESB highest 5 Star rating for the fifth consecutive year.
- Achieved distributable income from operations of \$46.2 million for the quarter from 1 July 2024 to 30 September 2024. DPU to unitholders for the quarter amounted to 1.580 cents.

### DEC

- Suntec REIT maintained GRESB 'A' rating for Public Disclosure.
- Mandatory conditional cash offer for all issued and outstanding units in Suntec REIT by Aelios Pte. Ltd.<sup>1</sup>

#### Note:

<sup>1</sup> The offer has lapsed. Please refer to the announcement dated 7 February 2025 for further details.

## Letter to Unitholders

![Portrait of a woman in a yellow floral qipao, likely a representative of Suntec REIT.](10953d657a5f47fdc829a800419dd370_img.jpg)

Portrait of a woman in a yellow floral qipao, likely a representative of Suntec REIT.

### Dear Unitholders

On behalf of the Board of ESR Trust Management (Suntec) Limited ("Board"), it is my pleasure to present to you the annual report of Suntec REIT for the financial year ended 31 December 2024 ("FY 2024").

2024 was another challenging year as geo-political tensions continue to rise while interest rates remained at elevated levels. Despite the strong headwinds, our portfolio of quality assets in strategic locations across Singapore, Australia and the United Kingdom enabled Suntec REIT to deliver a set of resilient performance in 2024.

## RESILIENT PERFORMANCE ANCHORED BY SINGAPORE PROPERTIES

Gross revenue increased marginally by 0.2% to \$463.6 million whilst net property income declined 0.8% to \$310.8 million. Income contributions from joint ventures increased 6.4% to \$100.0 million. These were mainly driven by improvements in the performance of our Singapore Office and Retail portfolios, as well as the Sydney properties, partially offset by vacancies in 55 Currie Street, Adelaide and The Minster Building, London.

Suntec REIT's total distributable income for FY 2024 was \$180.9 million, 12.5% lower year-on-year, as a result of the completion of the capital distribution in 2023 and higher financing cost.

Our Singapore office portfolio continued to deliver robust sets of results, achieving 26 consecutive quarters of positive rent reversion with strong reversion of 10.3% recorded for 2024.

The office towers at Suntec City Office, One Raffles Quay and Marina Bay Financial Centre achieved strong committed occupancies of above 98.0% as at 31 December 2024, above the market occupancy of 94.7% for Core CBD offices.

Our Australian portfolio remained stable with positive rent reversions for the year. Committed occupancies for our Australian portfolio remained healthy at 90.9%, higher than the nationwide CBD office occupancy of 84.8%. 177 Pacific Highway, Sydney, 21 Harris Street, Sydney and Olderfleet, 477 Collins Street, Melbourne had committed occupancy of 100% while the committed occupancy for

Southgate Complex, Melbourne was 90.1%. At 55 Currie Street in Adelaide, while the committed occupancy improved 5.2 percentage points to 61.4%, backfilling challenges remained due to the weak market demand.

In the United Kingdom, committed occupancy at Nova Properties remained stable at 99.6% while the committed occupancy at The Minster Building stood at 90.8%.

On the retail front, Suntec City Mall recorded another year of outstanding performance in FY 2024 with improvements across key operating indicators. The mall achieved a strong full-year rent reversion of 23.2% while committed occupancy improved to 98.4%. Through continuous efforts to curate a diverse tenant mix and collaboration with strategic partners, Suntec City remains the mall of choice amongst shoppers. Mall traffic grew 6.2% while tenant sales remained stable.

Suntec Convention continued its growth momentum with income recording an increase of 16.9% driven by improvement in operations and an increase in smaller but high yielding events.

## ASSETS UNDER MANAGEMENT

As at end 2024, Suntec REIT's assets under management ("AUM") was \$12.1 billion, a slight decline of 0.8%. This was mainly due to lower property values in Australia and the United Kingdom due to expansion of capitalisation rates, partly mitigated by the uplift in the valuation of the Singapore properties as a result of better operating performance. Suntec REIT continues to be Singapore-centric with 77.9% of its AUM in Singapore, with the remaining 12.4% and 9.7% in Australia and the United Kingdom respectively.

## OUR BALANCE SHEET

Suntec REIT remains focused on proactive capital management. In 2024, loans amounting to \$950.0 million were refinanced, enabling the REIT to achieve interest savings of \$3.1 million per annum.

As at 31 December 2024, the aggregate leverage ratio ("ALR") was 42.4% while the average financing cost for FY 2024 was 4.06% per annum with approximately 58.0% of the debt fixed or hedged. There is adequate headroom to the ALR limit of 50.0%.

<!-- PAGE 13 -->

To further strengthen our balance sheet, we divested \$58.3 million of strata units at Suntec City Office Towers at an average price of 24.5% above book value. The proceeds were used to pare down debts. The transactions were accretive to Suntec REIT's earnings as the achieved divestment yields were lower than the current borrowing costs.

## **SUSTAINABILITY — A CORNERSTONE OF OUR BUSINESS**

Suntec REIT is fully committed to sustainable operations and sound practices in areas of Environment, Social and Governance ("ESG"). Since embarking on the REIT's sustainability reporting journey in FY 2017, Suntec REIT had consistently achieved and maintained strong accreditation and accolades.

We are pleased to report that Suntec REIT attained the highest GRESB 5 Star rating for the fifth consecutive year since our inaugural participation in 2020. Suntec REIT also maintained 'A' for its public disclosure. GRESB is one of the leading ESG benchmarks for real estate and infrastructure investments globally.

In line with our net carbon zero roadmap and commitment towards sustainable growth, 21 Harris Street, achieved carbon neutral status in 2024, one year ahead of schedule. We have also managed to secure approximately 70% of our debts in green or sustainability-linked loans as at end December 2024.

More information can be found in our Sustainability Report, which will be available in electronic form on SGXNet and our website by end-May 2025.

## **OUTLOOK**

Geopolitical tensions, uncertainty over US trade policies and a potential slowdown in global growth are likely to weigh on office demand. Positive rent reversion for our Singapore office portfolio is expected to continue within a modest range of 1% to 5%, with revenue strengthening on the back of past quarters of positive rent reversion and healthy occupancies.

Revenue performance from Suntec City Mall is expected to improve, underpinned by positive rent reversion which is expected to be in the range of 10% to 15% with high committed occupancy of more than 95.0%. Suntec City Mall is well poised for future growth, supported by higher occupancy, rent and marcoms activities.

The Singapore MICE market is expected to continue its growth momentum, boosted by the growth in MICE activities. The performance of Suntec Convention is expected to be stable with the composition of event types likely to remain largely unchanged.

Our Australian portfolio is expected to remain stable supported by healthy occupancies of the Sydney and Melbourne properties with vacancy at 55 Currie Street likely to increase due to the weak market demand in Adelaide.

In the United Kingdom, the portfolio is underpinned by high occupancy and long weighted average lease expiry. However, leasing downtime at The Minster Building from vacancies is expected to impact the portfolio performance.

On the capital management front, while interest rates are expected to ease gradually, Suntec REIT's all-in financing cost is likely to increase by 10 to 20 basis points as interest rate hedges that are expiring in the year were secured at significantly lower rates.

Suntec REIT is one of the pioneer real estate investment trusts in Singapore. Over the last 20 years, we have navigated through different challenges and have emerged stronger and affirmed our position as one of the leading real estate investment trusts in Singapore.

As Suntec REIT continues its remarkable journey, we can look ahead with confidence. Suntec REIT's sound fundamentals and unwavering focus to create value for unitholders, underpinned by our diversified portfolio of high-quality assets and resilient income streams will put us in a good momentum for our next phase.

### **IN APPRECIATION**

On behalf of the Board and the Manager, I would like to thank Mr Lim Hwee Chiang, John who has stepped down as Non-Executive Director of the Manager. Mr Lim has served with distinction, providing insightful guidance and strategic counsel that has been instrumental to the growth and success of Suntec REIT.

We are pleased to welcome Mr Matthew James Lawson, who joined the Board as Non-Executive Director of the Manager. His extensive experience will certainly help to complement and strengthen the Board.

I would also like to thank my fellow Board members for their continued counsel and the management team for their hard work and dedication. Last but not least, I would like to extend my sincere appreciation to our unitholders, tenants, business partners and stakeholders for their continued trust and steadfast support.

### **CHEW GEK KHIM PJG**

Chairman and Non-Executive Director  
21 March 2025

For FY 2024, Suntec REIT's unit opening price was \$1.24 and closed at \$1.17 with a market capitalisation of \$3.4 billion as at 31 December 2024. Suntec REIT's FY 2024 DPU yield of 5.29% has also outperformed the Singapore Government 10-year bond yield at 2.86%. As at end FY 2024, Suntec REIT unitholders would have achieved a total return of 196.7% since listing. As one of Singapore's most liquid listed REITs, the overall traded volume was 2,192 million units for the 12 months ended 31 December 2024. Suntec REIT is also a constituent member of major global indices such as the MSCI Global Small Cap Index, FTSE EPRA Nareit Global Real Estate Index and the Global Property Research 250 Index series. It is also a constituent of the FTSE Straits Times Mid Cap Index and FTSE Straits Times Real Estate Index in Singapore.

## Trust Structure

![Trust Structure Diagram](1a827b10290f33d4fec04d0e8ef7a897_img.jpg)

The diagram illustrates the Trust Structure of Suntec REIT. At the top, **Unitholders** provide an **Investment in Suntec REIT** and receive a **Distribution**. Below them is **SUNTEC Real Estate Investment Trust**, which is managed by **ESR Trust Management (Suntec) Limited**. ESR Trust Management provides **Asset Management Services** to Suntec REIT and receives **Fees**. Suntec REIT acts for Unitholders and receives **Fees** from **HSBC Institutional Trust Services (Singapore) Limited (REIT Trustee)**. Suntec REIT owns assets and provides **Net Property Income and Income Contribution** to the **Suntec REIT Portfolio**. The Suntec REIT Portfolio is managed by **Property Managers** who receive **Fees** and provide **Property Management Services**. The Suntec REIT Portfolio is divided into three regions: **Singapore**, **Australia**, and **United Kingdom**.

**Singapore**

- Suntec City
- Suntec Singapore<sup>1</sup> (66.3% interest)
- One Raffles Quay (one-third interest)
- MBFC Properties<sup>2</sup> (one-third interest)

**Australia**

- 177 Pacific Highway, Sydney
- 21 Harris Street, Sydney
- Southgate Complex, Melbourne (50.0% interest)
- Olderfleet, 477 Collins Street, Melbourne (50.0% interest)
- 55 Currie Street, Adelaide

**United Kingdom**

- Nova Properties<sup>3</sup> (50.0% interest)
- The Minster Building

Trust Structure Diagram

# Manager's Report

### YEAR IN REVIEW

Suntec REIT achieved a total distributable income of \$180.9 million and DPU of 6.192 cents for the financial year ended 31 December 2024 ("FY 2024"). As at end FY 2024, Suntec REIT's assets under management ("AUM") stood at approximately \$12.1 billion, underpinned by a 4.4 million square feet ("sq ft") of office portfolio and 1.0 million sq ft of retail portfolio strategically located in the prime districts of Singapore, Australia and the United Kingdom.

## FINANCIAL PERFORMANCE

Suntec REIT achieved gross revenue of \$463.6 million in FY 2024 which was 0.2% higher compared to the corresponding period in 2023 ("FY 2023"). This was mainly due to higher contribution from Suntec City Office and Suntec City Mall. Lower revenue from the Australian portfolio and The Minster Building offset the higher contribution.

The net property income for FY 2024 was \$310.8 million, a slight decrease of 0.8% year-on-year, as higher revenue attained was offset by absence of one-off property tax refund at Suntec City Mall.

The total income contribution from joint ventures, excluding gain/loss on change in fair value adjustments, was \$100.0 million. This mainly comprised share of profits (excluding net change in fair value of investment properties) and interest income from loans to joint ventures (if any) of \$30.2 million from One Raffles Quay, \$38.8 million from MBFC Properties, \$2.4 million from Southgate Complex and \$28.6 million from Nova Properties.

The total income contribution from joint ventures for FY 2024 was 6.4% higher mainly due to stronger operating performance at MBFC Properties and One Raffles Quay, as well as higher contribution from Nova Properties due to reversal of impairment of receivables.

Suntec REIT's distributable income of \$180.9 million attained in FY 2024 was 12.5% lower year-on-year. This was mainly due to the completion of capital distribution of \$23.0 million in FY 2023 and higher financing costs arising from higher interest rates. This was partially mitigated by the higher net property income on better operating performance from the Singapore and Sydney properties and higher dividend contribution from Suntec Singapore. The DPU for FY 2024 was 6.192 cents, 13.2% lower year-on-year.

In FY 2024, the total rent guarantee received amounted to approximately \$1.5 million. This translated to 0.051 cents of FY 2024 DPU.

#### CAPITAL STRUCTURE

Suntec REIT's total consolidated debt stood at \$4,227 million, with an aggregated leverage ratio of 42.4% as at 31 December 2024. The all-in-cost of financing of Suntec REIT's debt portfolio for FY 2024 was 4.06% per annum.

In FY 2024, Suntec REIT refinanced loans amounting to \$950 million with sustainability-linked loans.

Suntec REIT's exposure to derivatives is elaborated in the Financial Statements. The fair value of derivatives for FY 2024, which is included in the Financial Statements as "Derivative assets" and "Derivative liabilities", was \$12.3 million and \$12.9 million respectively. The net fair value of derivatives represented 0.01% of the net assets of Suntec REIT as at 31 December 2024.

<!-- PAGE 24 -->

## Manager's Report

## PROPERTY PORTFOLIO

In Singapore, to drive traffic and sales, strong brand partnerships and loyalty partnerships were forged at Suntec City Mall. With these partnerships in place, Suntec City Mall aimed to meet shoppers' desire for value and convenience, and improve mindshare amongst shoppers.

To maintain One Raffles Quay's standing as the preferred choice amongst tenants, the Garden Plaza and main lobbies at North and South Tower have undergone asset enhancement to elevate the arrival experience. Additional Food and Beverage offerings were also introduced and were well-received by tenants.

In Australia, asset enhancement works at 55 Currie Street and 177 Pacific Highway to revitalise the workspaces and elevate the office environment have been completed and were well received by office tenants.

In navigating the current environment of elevated interest rates, \$58.3 million<sup>1</sup> of strata units at Suntec City Office Towers were divested at an average price of 24.5% above book value. The proceeds were used to pare down debts. The transactions are expected to be accretive to Suntec REIT's earnings as the achieved yields were lower than the current borrowing costs.

Suntec REIT's property portfolio comprising approximately 4.4 million sq ft of office space and more than 1.0 million sq ft of retail and convention space was valued at \$11.8 billion or 1.2% lower than the preceding year. This was mainly due to the lower valuations of the Australia and the United Kingdom portfolios, though mitigated by the increase in valuation of the Singapore portfolio. The net asset value of Suntec REIT and its subsidiaries stood at \$2.05 per unit as at 31 December 2024.

#### Notes:

- 1 The transactions in respect of the sale of strata units amounting to \$58.3 million<sup>2</sup> to separate unrelated third party investors namely Felix Petroleum Pte. Ltd., Internet Sharing Pte. Ltd., Tayen Investment Pte. Ltd. and Nuodasi International Trading Pte. Ltd. were entered into in FY2024. The sale of strata units amounting to \$41.9 million, were completed in FY 2024 while the balance strata unit sale was completed on 6 January 2025.
- 2 Based on the valuation of \$47.1 million derived by multiplying the Rate of Lettable Floor Area (\$ per square metre) per the 31 December 2023 (in respect of the \$41.9 million divested in FY2024) and 31 December 2024 (in respect of the balance strata unit sale completed on 6 January 2025) independent valuation reports by the net lettable area of the divested strata units, using the income capitalisation, discounted cashflow and direct comparison methods.

### STRONG OCCUPANCY

Suntec REIT's asset portfolio performance continued to remain strong. As at 31 December 2024, the Singapore office portfolio achieved an overall committed occupancy of 98.7%, or 4.0 percentage points higher than the overall Core CBD occupancy of 94.7%<sup>1</sup>. For the Singapore retail portfolio, the overall committed occupancy as at 31 December 2024 was 98.3%.

In Australia, the office portfolio achieved an overall committed occupancy of 90.9%, or 6.1 percentage points higher than the nationwide CBD occupancy of 84.8%<sup>2</sup>.

In the United Kingdom, the overall portfolio achieved a committed occupancy of 95.1%, 3.7 percentage points higher than the Central London Office occupancy of 91.4%<sup>2</sup>.

Suntec REIT's overall committed occupancy for the office and retail portfolio stood at 95.4% and 97.9% respectively as at 31 December 2024.

## Property Portfolio

### HIGH QUALITY COMMERCIAL ASSETS

**Strategically Located in Prime Districts of Singapore, Australia and the United Kingdom**

Suntec REIT's portfolio comprises prime commercial properties in Suntec City, 66.3% interest in Suntec Singapore Convention & Exhibition Centre ("Suntec Singapore"), one-third interest in One Raffles Quay and one-third interest in Marina Bay Financial Centre Towers 1 and 2 and the Marina Bay Link Mall ("MBLM" and collectively known as "MBFC Properties"). These properties are located within Singapore's Central Business District ("CBD"), Marina Bay and the Civic and Cultural District. In Australia, Suntec REIT holds a 100% interest in 177 Pacific Highway and 21 Harris Street, both located in Sydney, 50.0% interest in Southgate Complex and 50.0% interest in Olderfleet, 477 Collins Street, both located in Melbourne, as well as 100% interest in 55 Currie Street in Adelaide. In the United Kingdom, Suntec REIT owns a 50.0% interest in Nova North, Nova South and The Nova Building (collectively known as "Nova Properties") and 100% interest in The Minster Building which are both located in London.

Spanning a total net lettable area ("NLA") of approximately 5.4 million square feet ("sq ft"), the properties provide a steady stream of income from a well-diversified pool of strong office and retail tenants.

#### DIVERSE TENANT MIX

Suntec REIT's office portfolio leases are well diversified across more than 13 business sectors. 52.1% of the total gross office rental income for the month of December 2024 was attributable to the major business sectors of Technology, Media and Telecommunications, and Banking, Insurance and Financial Services. The top 10 tenants of the office portfolio contributed 19.8% of Suntec REIT's total office gross rental income for the month of December 2024 and occupied an area representing 21.9% of the REIT's total office portfolio area.

For the retail portfolio, 55.1% of the total gross retail rental income for the month of December 2024 was attributable to the major business sectors of Food and Beverage and Fashion and Accessories. The top 10 tenants of the retail portfolio contributed 15.9% of Suntec REIT's total gross retail income for the month of December 2024 and occupied an area representing 27.7% of the REIT's total retail portfolio area.

### LEASE EXPIRY PROFILE

In FY 2024, approximately 663,917 sq ft of office space was renewed and replaced, including forward renewal of approximately 270,735 sq ft of the office leases expiring in FY 2025 and FY 2026.

As at 31 December 2024, 45.4% of the total office NLA is due to expire during the period from FY 2025 to FY 2027, while 50.0% is due to expire in FY 2028 and beyond.

For the retail portfolio as at 31 December 2024, 73.1% of the total retail NLA is due to expire during the period from FY 2025 to FY 2027, while 24.8% is due to expire in FY 2028 and beyond.

#### WEIGHTED AVERAGE LEASE EXPIRY PROFILE

The weighted average lease expiry ("WALE") of the overall office portfolio was 3.81 years at 31 December 2024. The Singapore and overseas office portfolios' WALE was 2.36 years and 5.28 years respectively. The WALE of the office leases committed in FY 2024 was 3.49 years. These leases contribute 17.6% to the total monthly gross office rental income.

The WALE of the overall retail portfolio was 2.29 years as at 31 December 2024. The Singapore and overseas retail portfolios' WALE was 2.25 years and 3.08 years respectively. The WALE of the retail leases committed in FY 2024 was 2.83 years. These leases contribute 47.3% to the total monthly gross retail rental income.

<!-- PAGE 31 -->

Suntec City is an iconic integrated commercial development located in the Marina Central Business Improvement District.

A premier business, MICE<sup>1</sup>, shopping and lifestyle destination, Suntec City comprises five Grade A office towers, one of Singapore's largest shopping malls and a world-class convention and exhibition centre. The development is interlinked by street level plazas and underground walkways, with the iconic Fountain of Wealth nestled in the heart of the development.

Suntec City Office Towers had achieved the Building and Construction Authority ("BCA") Green Mark Platinum

award. With this achievement, the entire Suntec City is now fully Green Mark compliant.

Suntec REIT owns 53.9% of Suntec City Office, 100% of Suntec City Mall, and 66.3% interest in Suntec Singapore Convention and Exhibition Centre ("Suntec Singapore"). Easily accessible by car and public transport, Suntec City houses more than 3,000 carpark lots over two basement levels. It is directly connected to the Promenade and Esplanade Mass Rapid Transit ("MRT") stations and is also a 5-minute walk to City Hall MRT station.

**The Manager's objective for Suntec City is to generate sustainable growth for the office, retail and convention businesses.**

Suntec REIT owns a net lettable area (“NLA”) of approximately 1.2 million sq ft of Suntec City Office, comprising strata units across Towers 1, 2 and 3, and all strata units in Towers 4 and 5. Towers 1 to 4 are 45-storey buildings with typical floor plates ranging from 10,000 sq ft to 14,000 sq ft, whilst Tower 5 is an 18-storey building with large floor plates of about 28,000 sq ft.

With good building specifications and a strong ecosystem, Suntec City Office attracts a good stream of diverse multinational firms from sectors such as Technology, Media and Telecommunications, Banking, Insurance and Financial Services and Real Estate and Property Services.

#### DIVERSE TENANT MIX

For the month of December 2024, 38.9% of the total gross office rental income was attributable to the Technology, Media and Telecommunications sector, followed by 13.1% and 8.7% from the Banking, Insurance and Financial Services sector and Real Estate and Property Services sector respectively.

#### SUNTEC CITY MALL

Suntec City houses over 360 retail establishments featuring a wide range of speciality stores, food and beverage options and entertainment concepts.

In addition to local residents and tourists, the mall caters to the needs of the working population in and around Suntec City, as well as the vast network of local and international delegates who convene at Suntec Singapore for exhibitions, events and conferences.

<!-- PAGE 35 -->

#### EVENTS AT SUNTEC CITY

Throughout the year, Suntec City hosted a myriad of activities in the mall. These include brand activations and roadshows at our atrium and event spaces. Strategic partnerships with LEGO® and POP BEAN for mall campaigns and festive celebrations also injected a vibrant atmosphere and delighted our shoppers.

Suntec City continued to celebrate the Purple Parade – a unifying national platform to promote awareness and celebrate the abilities of Persons with Disabilities. The 2024 edition saw a record turnout of more than 15,000 people and was the ninth consecutive year that Suntec City was the supporting partner for Purple Parade.

#### Property Details

#### SUNTEC SINGAPORE CONVENTION & EXHIBITION CENTRE

![Exterior view of the Suntec Singapore Convention & Exhibition Centre at night, illuminated with blue and white lights. The building features a large, illuminated 'SUNTEC CITY' logo and a 'DONKI' store visible in the ground floor.](6ec000d1ceaaffb3eb238ea0d61f3c5c_img.jpg)

A night-time photograph of the Suntec Singapore Convention & Exhibition Centre. The building's facade is illuminated with blue and white lights, highlighting its modern architecture. The 'SUNTEC CITY' logo is prominently displayed in the center. Below the main entrance, a 'DONKI' store is visible. The sky is dark blue, and the overall atmosphere is vibrant and modern.

Exterior view of the Suntec Singapore Convention & Exhibition Centre at night, illuminated with blue and white lights. The building features a large, illuminated 'SUNTEC CITY' logo and a 'DONKI' store visible in the ground floor.

Suntec Singapore Convention & Exhibition Centre ("Suntec Singapore") is a world-class meeting, convention and exhibition venue.

The award-winning facility offers flexible and customisable spaces that can cater to events for 10 to 10,000 attendees. The venue has direct access to approximately 5,800 hotel rooms, 1,000 retail outlets, 300 restaurants and is within close proximity to Singapore's entertainment and cultural attractions.

Since 1995, Suntec Singapore has hosted many notable events such as the World Trade Organization Ministerial Meetings in 1996, the Annual Meetings of the Board of Governors of the International Monetary Fund and World Bank Group in 2006, the Leaders Week in 2009, the 33rd ASEAN Summit Meetings in 2018 and the Tour De France Singapore Criterium in 2023. Suntec Singapore also served as one of the largest sporting venues for the inaugural Youth Olympic Games in 2010 and the Olympic Esports Week in 2023.

The continued recovery of tourism supported the return of more international and regional MICE events, while domestic demand for corporate and consumer events remained strong. FY 2024 saw 16 new-to-Suntec and new-to-Singapore events, boosted by the efforts of the Singapore Tourism Board to position Singapore as the "World's Best MICE City".

<!-- PAGE 37 -->

Suntec Singapore's commitment to clients and partners was well appreciated and was demonstrated through it clinching various awards, such as the World MICE Award in 2024 for Singapore's Best Convention Centre and the World Travel Award in 2024 for the World's Leading Hi-Tech Meetings and Convention Centre.

#### ONE RAFFLES QUAY, SINGAPORE

![A nighttime photograph of One Raffles Quay, a tall, modern office building with a glass facade, illuminated from within. The building is situated in a dense urban environment with other skyscrapers visible in the background. The sky is a deep blue, and the city lights create a vibrant atmosphere. A nighttime photograph of One Raffles Quay, a tall, modern office building with a glass facade, illuminated from within. The building is situated in a dense urban environment with other skyscrapers visible in the background. The sky is a deep blue, and the city lights create a vibrant atmosphere. One Raffles Quay is a prime landmark commercial development strategically located in Marina Bay.

Designed by internationally renowned architectural firm Kohn Pedersen Fox Associates of New York, One Raffles Quay comprises a 50-storey office tower (the “North Tower”) and a 29-storey office tower (the “South Tower”). An underground pedestrian network connects the development directly to the Downtown and Raffles Place MRT stations and the major buildings within Marina Bay and Raffles Place. The development has a sheltered plaza serving as a drop-off point and a hub car park with 713 car park lots.

21 Harris Street is a nine storey campus style building comprising of a café, childcare, gymnasium, basement carpark and end-of-trip facility.

The Pyrmont precinct has evolved into Sydney's technology media and entertainment hub and the property is within a 10-minute drive or 15 to 20 minutes' walk to Sydney core CBD. It is also next to John Street Light Rail Station which is a 15 minutes' direct ride to Sydney's Central Train Station.

The New South Wales government announced it would build a new metro railway at Pyrmont as part of the Sydney Metro West project which would shorten the travel time to Sydney CBD to two minutes.

Key tenants at 21 Harris Street include, Medium Rare Content Agency, Publicis Groupe, Strandbags Group and Viva Leisure.

The building is Carbon Neutral certified by NABERS and has a 6 Star Green Star Rating, 5 Star NABERS Energy Rating and a 3.5 star NABERS Water Rating.

Southgate Complex is a freehold, landmark waterfront integrated development located alongside the Yarra River in the Southbank arts and leisure precinct of Melbourne. Southgate Complex comprises two Grade A office towers, the 30-storey “IBM Tower”, the 25-storey “HWT Centre”, a 3-storey retail podium and a car park with 975 lots.

Southgate Complex is directly opposite Flinders Street train station and within close proximity to Melbourne's city rail loop. It is also surrounded by business, residential, recreational and retail amenities.

Southgate Complex has a premier tenant base with major office tenants including Alinta, IBM Australia, JB Hi-Fi and The Herald and Weekly Times.

IBM Tower has a 4.5 Star NABERS Energy Rating while HWT Centre has a 3.5 Star NABERS Energy Rating and 4 Star NABERS Water Rating.

On 31 May 2018, Suntec REIT increased its interest in Southgate Complex from 25.0% to 50.0%. The remaining 50.0% is owned by PIP Trust.

In end 2024, new third spaces such as a business lounge, a wellness room and a reflection room were created to improve the attractiveness of the property, and cater to the evolving needs of occupiers. The completion of these third spaces are expected in the first half of 2025.

Planning control amendments for the potential redevelopment of the retail podium and construction of a new office tower have been approved by relevant authorities in Melbourne in 2021. The Manager is reevaluating the feasibility of the redevelopment in view of the economic uncertainty.

The development achieved 6 Star Green Star rating, 5.5 Star NABERS Energy rating, 5 Star NABERS Water rating as well as International WELL Platinum rating for Core & Shell. The property provides market leading amenities including a business lounge, fitness studio, food and beverage options and hotel-style end-of-trip facilities and concierge services.

The property is adjacent to the 5-Star Intercontinental Melbourne Hotel, and is located within the Melbourne CBD Free Tram Zone. In addition to the four tram lines serving Collins Street, the Southern Cross Station, Victoria's primary metropolitan and regional transportation hub is also a short walking distance away.

In recognition of its architectural excellence, Olderfleet, 477 Collins Street won the prestigious World Architecture Festival (WAF) 2021 Awards for Office — Completed Buildings category and awarded winner for the Best Tall Building 100–199 metres category at the Council on Tall Building and Urban Habitat conference in 2022.

Olderfleet, 477 Collins Street premier tenant base includes Deloitte, Lander & Rogers, Norton Rose, Urbis and Workclub.

In equal partnership with leading diversified Australian property group, Mirvac, Suntec REIT holds 50.0% interest in the property.

The Nova Properties consists of two Grade A office buildings with ancillary retail and is located in the heart of Victoria, West End, London. Completed in 2016 and 2017, the Nova Properties was constructed to the highest standards and is the newest large scale addition to Victoria, West End.

Suntec REIT made its maiden foray into London with the acquisition of Nova Properties. The asset is a strategic fit with Suntec REIT's existing portfolio of high quality commercial assets in Singapore and Australia.

Nova Properties is near to key landmarks such as the Buckingham Palace, Westminster Abbey and the Houses of Parliament. The buildings are also situated opposite the Victoria Station, an important interchange for the London Underground network and Victoria Coach station.

Key tenants at Nova Properties include Advent International, Atkins, The Argyll Club, a government agency and Vitol. In terms of sustainability, Nova Properties has an Office BREEAM rating of "Very Good" and Retail BREEAM rating of "Good", EPC Rating of "B", as well as International WELL Platinum rating for Core & Shell.

In equal partnership with Landsec, one of the largest commercial property development and investment companies in the United Kingdom, Suntec REIT holds 50.0% interest in the property.

The Minster Building is a Grade A office building strategically located within City of London's central business district with extensive connection to London's transportation network. The 11-storey property had undergone significant refurbishment which was completed in 2018.

The Minster Building was Suntec REIT's second acquisition into London and the asset is a strategic fit with Suntec REIT's existing portfolio and enhances the resilience, diversification and income stability of Suntec REIT's portfolio.

The property is surrounded by several important institutions of historical significance and landmark attractions such as Lloyd's of London, The Bank of England, The Royal Exchange, Leadenhall Market, The Tower of London and Tower Bridge. The Minster Building is also within walking distance to Monument, Tower Hill and Liverpool train stations.

Key tenants at The Minster Building include ADM Investor Services Inc, Ardonagh Specialty Limited, Lyst, Risk Management Solutions Limited and Trustpilot. In terms of sustainability, The Minster Building has an Office BREEAM rating of "Very Good" and EPC Rating of "B".

#### TENANT MIX

For the month of December 2024, 42.3% of the total gross rental income was attributable to the Banking, Insurance and Financial Services sector.

With the long lease tenures, 5.9% of the total NLA are due to expire in FY 2025 with the balance 84.9% only due to expire in FY 2028 and beyond.

## SINGAPORE OFFICE AND RETAIL MARKET

### 1. ECONOMIC OVERVIEW

The Ministry of Trade and Industry (MTI) reported that Singapore's GDP grew by 4.4% year-on-year (y-o-y) in 2024, an increase of 2.6 percentage points (pps) from 2023. The manufacturing sector rebounded with 4.3% growth, driven by strong performances in electronics, transport engineering, and general manufacturing. The finance & insurance sector saw significant growth, supported by higher trading activity, increased credit intermediation, and robust fees and commissions in banking and fund management. However, certain consumer-facing sectors like retail trade and food & beverage services struggled, with local consumers shifting their spending towards overseas travel.

Looking ahead in 2025, Singapore's GDP growth is expected to moderate, as external demand softens amid global uncertainties. The manufacturing and trade-related services sectors will continue to grow, though at a slower pace, while the electronics cluster should remain resilient due to strong semiconductor demand. Finance & insurance and information & communications sectors are expected to maintain healthy growth. However, risks remain, including global trade frictions, geopolitical tensions, and potential financial instability due to prolonged tight monetary conditions.

Headline consumer price inflation (CPI) in Singapore is recorded at 2.4% in 2024, down significantly from 4.8% in 2023. This decline is attributed to moderating global inflationary pressures and the appreciation of the Singapore dollar, which helped lower the cost of imported goods.

Foreign Direct Investment (FDI) is a critical driver of Singapore's economic growth. In 2023, FDI flows into Singapore totalled S\$214 billion, marking a 10.2% y-o-y increase from 2022 and a compound annual growth rate (CAGR) of 10.0% since 2019. Based on CAGR, the top-performing sectors were Transportation & Storage (69.7%), Real Estate (22.6%), and Finance & Insurance (16.1%). The 2023 y-o-y growth was primarily driven by the Finance & Insurance sector, which saw a 64.2% increase, and the Real Estate sector, with a significant 107.8% rebound after a contraction in 2022.

### 2. OVERVIEW OF OFFICE MARKET

#### 2.1 Demand, Supply and Occupancy

According to estimates from the Urban Redevelopment Authority (URA), Singapore's total net office stock amounted to 74.3 million sq ft as of Q4 2024. Of this, 36.4 million sq ft (49%) is located within the Downtown Core planning area, and the remaining in the Fringe Area (24.4%), Orchard (3.8%), Rest of Central Region (13.3%) and Outside Central Region (9.4%). In 2024, the completion of IOI Central Boulevard Towers added approximately 1.1 million sq ft of office space, with Amazon and Morgan Stanley serving as its anchor tenants. Another key addition to the office landscape is Labrador Tower, which offers 600,000 sq ft of office space and is part of the Greater Southern Waterfront development.

Nationwide, net new office supply amounted to 695,000 sq ft in 2024, with overall occupancy rising to 89.4%. The gradual absorption of new CBD office supply has led to a decline in downtown core vacancy rates, easing from 10.7% in Q3 2024 to 9.8% in Q4 2024.

Advanced estimates by Oxford Economics forecast employment growth in 2024, driven by a 2.1% increase in white-collar jobs, led by the technology and financial services sectors. This positive outlook is expected to sustain steady demand for office space, particularly as firms reinforce their work-from-office policies.

#### 2.2 Supply Pipeline

According to data from URA, the pipeline of net lettable office space currently under construction and expected to be completed between 2025 and 2029 is approximately 2.9 million sq ft. This represents an average annual supply of 571,000 sq ft over the next five years. Key office projects set for completion in 2025 includes Keppel South Central (521,000 sq ft) and the redevelopment of Shaw Towers (476,000 sq ft). Keppel South Central has secured financial institution Manulife as its anchor tenant. Office space supply between 2025 and 2029 is expected to be limited, with most new projects under construction located within the Central Business District (CBD). Several of these projects are redevelopment projects, such as Solitaire on Cecil (184,000 sq ft) and Newport Tower, the former Fuji Xerox Towers (218,900 sq ft).

Source: Colliers, URA Realis

#### 2.3 Rental Performance

According to URA and Colliers' estimates, average island wide office rents reached S\$6.49 per sq ft per month in Q4 2024, marking a 0.8% y-o-y increase. Grade A office rents in the CBD and City Fringe continued to grow, rising by 1.7% to S\$11.68 per sq ft per month in Q4 2024, while City Fringe rents saw a modest increase of 0.1% to S\$8.13 per sq ft per month. Grade B office rents in the CBD and Grade A Suburban office rent recorded the highest growth, rising by 1.8% to S\$9.12 per sq ft per month and 2.5% to S\$5.29 per sq ft per month, respectively.

#### 2.4 Outlook

The office market may experience heightened activity as lower borrowing costs, resulting from anticipated interest rate cuts, could relieve the financial pressure on certain companies and encourage office space take-up.

Hybrid work arrangements, which gained significant traction during the pandemic, are beginning to wane as

organizations increasingly recognize the value of working onsite for fostering innovation, collaboration, and social interaction. This is evident in resilient office rents and decreasing overall vacancy rates, particularly in core CBD areas. Companies such as Grab and Amazon have already implemented a complete work-from-office policy, while those maintaining a hybrid model are considering following suit based on their business needs.

However, tenants vacating older properties for new completions might result in an increase in secondary stock, leading to some volatility in vacancy rates. The expected completion of Shaw Towers in the CBD area by mid to late 2025 and the anticipated rise in secondary stock will likely exert near-term pressure on the broader CBD. Nonetheless, sustained demand, driven by Singapore's strategic position in regional and global markets provides a stable outlook for the Singapore office market, Colliers envisages prime office rents to grow moderately from 0% to 2% in 2025.

# Independent Market Report

### 3. OVERVIEW OF RETAIL MARKET

#### 3.1 Retail Sales Performance

According to the Department of Statistics, total retail sales for FY2024 excluding motor vehicles reached S\$42.3 billion, a slight 1.0% y-o-y decline from 2023. Online sales contributed approximately 12.4% of total retail sales, excluding motor vehicles. The highest share of online sales occurred in November, driven by major shopping events such as 11.11 and Black Friday promotions across various platforms.

All sectors recorded a y-o-y decline in sales except for Supermarkets & Hypermarkets, Food & Alcohol, Cosmetics, Toiletries & Medical Goods and Watches & Jewellery. Food & Alcohol recorded the highest y-o-y growth of 8.4% while Wearing Apparel & Footwear (-5.7%), Computer & Telecommunications Equipment (-6.5%) and Optical Goods & Books (-5.6%) saw a y-o-y decline.

The trend of higher retail sales levels towards the end of the year remained similar as per preceding years. The year-end festivities continued recovery of tourism, and lunar new year purchases have likely encouraged higher

retail sales. However as compared to December 2023, lower retail sales were recorded in December 2024. A higher level of outbound departures of Singapore residents were observed in December 2024, which could have contributed to overall lower retail spending by domestic consumers.

#### 3.2 Demand, Supply and Occupancy

As of Q4 2024, the total island wide retail stock reached over 51.5 million sq ft, a modest 1.6% growth compared to Q4 2023. Approximately 807,000 sq ft of new retail space was added in 2024, driven by the completion of notable developments such as Pasir Ris Mall (282,000 sq ft), Labrador Tower (28,000 sq. ft) and IOI Central Boulevard Tower (17,000 sq. ft). Net absorption continued to recover in 2024, increasing significantly by 57.3% y-o-y, compared to 2023, contributed by both new retailers and expansion of existing brands. Amidst higher operating costs and lower profit margins, several F&B establishments have been forced to wind up or relocate to areas with lower rents. Nonetheless, newly opened establishments and the influx of foreign sports and F&B brands outpace the closures and support demand for retail space.

#### 3.3 Supply Pipeline

According to URA data, Colliers estimates that over 518,000 sq ft of net lettable retail space will be added between 2025 and 2028. This translates to an average annual supply of 12,950 sq ft over the next four years, key retail developments slated for completion in 2025 includes Punggol Coast Mall (96,300 sq ft), The Cathay (81,800 sq ft) and Keppel South Central (27,600 sq ft). In the Central Region, there is a trend in the upcoming retail spaces being integrated with mixed-use developments such as

service apartments, hotels, offices, and residential units to support their primary functions. Additionally, several of these are redevelopment projects that reflect ongoing efforts by developers to update and maintain the relevance of their properties. Key projects include Shaw Towers (11,700 sq ft) and Newport Plaza, formerly Fuji Xerox Towers, which will add 3,400 sq ft of net lettable retail space to the pipeline.

<!-- PAGE 59 -->

#### 3.4 Retail Rental Indices

The retail rental market experienced significant fluctuations from 2019 to 2024. The URA Retail Rental Indices peaked in Q4 2019 before sharply declining due to weakened retail activity and economic disruptions caused by the pandemic. In 2024, indices for both the Central Area and the broader region showed minimal recovery compared to the previous year but still below pre-pandemic levels. In Q4 2024, the Central Area recorded a

0.6% quarter-on-quarter (q-o-q) growth, with a modest 0.5% y-o-y increase. Meanwhile, the Fringe Area, despite seeing 0.4% q-o-q growth, lagged 2023 levels with a 1.0% y-o-y decline. The resurgence of tourism had driven a 10% increase in tourism receipts, reaching S\$22.4 billion in 2024. In contrast, domestic spending remained stagnant as more consumers directed their expenditures overseas, led by the strong Singapore dollar.

#### 3.5 Outlook

Retailers continue to face challenges such as labour shortages, high operating costs, and increasing competition from e-commerce and live-streaming platforms. Many F&B retailers, traditionally operating on thin profit margins, are under pressure to scale down their business operations.

The ongoing return of employees to office settings is expected to boost footfall in retail malls located near offices, not only in the CBD but also in City Fringe and Suburban areas where decentralized offices are situated. International visitor arrivals have been steadily recovering, and a series of upcoming attractions, including Minion Land at Universal Studios Singapore and Rainforest Wild Asia in 2025, are projected to draw visitors and drive retail spending. Additionally, with the Singapore dollar expected to appreciate at a slower pace, residents may be encouraged to redirect overseas spending to domestic retailers.

Singapore continues to be an attractive market for global brands, evidenced by the increasing presence and expansion of Chinese brands. The sustained recovery in tourism, coupled with a robust pipeline of concerts and meetings, incentives, conference, and events (MICE) activities, is anticipated to support demand for retail space, particularly in prime retail malls. The Johor-Singapore Rapid Transit System (RTS) is expected to contribute to some outbound retail leakage, particularly impacting northern suburban malls and F&B retailers. However, prime retail malls in Singapore are likely to be less affected, with rental growth projected to range between 1% to 3% in 2025.

## AUSTRALIA OFFICE REVIEW

### 1. ECONOMIC OVERVIEW

In 2024, Australia's economy navigated a delicate balance between curbing inflation and sustaining growth. Following the Royal Bank of Australia's (RBA) monetary tightening, inflation fell from a peak of 7.8% to 2.4% by December 2024, prompting expectations of rate cuts as early as February 2025, with rates dropping to 3.4% to 3.9% by late 2025. Despite high interest rates weighing on consumption and housing, strong population growth supported GDP, which is forecast to rise from 1.5% in December 2024 to 2.3% December 2025.

Consumption growth is expected to pick up, albeit later than anticipated. Robust net overseas migration (NOM) will continue fuelling economic expansion, though at more normalised levels post-pandemic. Australia's population grew 2.1% y-o-y to June 2024 and is projected to rise

1.5% annually over the next two years. NOM is expected to average 240,000 people annually over the next decade, compared to the pre-pandemic ten-year average of 216,000.

Overall, Australia's economy remains resilient, with steady population-driven growth supporting labour markets and GDP expansion.

### 2. OVERVIEW OF AUSTRALIA OFFICE MARKET

An increased focus on employee experience has impacted office space decision making over the past 12 to 24 months, which had been mostly influenced by the immediate impacts of flexible working arrangements and efforts to bring employees back to the office post pandemic. As a result, leasing demand had been led by tenants relocating to improved locations and better-quality assets which has underpinned strong gross leasing activity. Total gross leasing volumes in 2024 was up by 15.1% compared to the prior year. As such, positive CBD net absorption of 954,360 sq ft was registered in 2024, a significant improvement from 2023 levels (-1.9 million sq ft). Over the year, the national CBD vacancy rate was fairly stable rising only 0.3 pps to 14.5%.

### 3. SYDNEY CBD OFFICE MARKET OUTLOOK

#### 3.1 Demand, Supply and Occupancy

Vacancy had increased in the latest Property Council of Australia (PCA) data rising 1.2pps to 12.8%. However, this was due to anticipated new speculative supply from the completion of the three Over Station Developments that were committed to be developed due to the timing of construction of the new metro station developments. Excluding tenant movement into new supply, there was positive take-up activity from tenants opening new offices in the CBD or expanding whilst relocating their premises to better quality assets and locations. Larger deals over 32,300 sq ft also gathered pace.

#### 3.2 Supply Pipeline

Sydney CBD is entering a period of below-average supply levels with 2025 earmarked to be 30% below the historical ten-year average. This shortfall is projected to increase to 60% in 2026. Over 2025, there are only two developments expected to enter stock totalling 500,520 sq ft. The largest being the major refurbishment of 33 Alfred Street (338,000 sq ft) which has had strong demand from legal tenants and looking to complete with a commitment rate of at least 85%.

#### 3.3 Rent and Incentives

Average prime net face rents increased 3.1% y-o-y, driven by a 5.0% increase within the Core precinct, as demand for well-located prime space rose. Average prime incentives remained stable at 35.0% over 2024, though a wide range existed across precincts. Average prime net effective rents remained stable at A\$63/sq ft per annum, though a divergence in the recovery of effective rents persisted across precincts.

#### 3.4 Investment Activity

Over 2024, 10 assets were transacted totalling A\$3.14 billion, doubling 2023 deal volumes. Sydney's transactional activity is firmly following the occupier thematic for higher

demand assets in core locations with half the assets sold and 70% of the value transacted over 2024 located in the Sydney CBD Core precinct. Whilst the largest transaction was attributed to Mitsui buying into the 55 Pitt Street development in the Sydney CBD, the largest existing standing asset transaction was the sale of 388 George Street for A\$460 million to Singapore Land JV UOL Group. The average prime yield softened 0.7pps over the year to 6.4%, with Q4 2024 highlighting a stabilisation of yields in the Sydney CBD with no further yield softening reported for the first time since September 2022.

#### 3.5 Outlook

With a lack of new Premium or high-end A grade supply in core locations being developed to meet the demand for higher quality space, we expect some tight conditions to start emerging by the end of 2025 within the prime end of the market. This will allow effective rents to recover as incentive levels start to taper where limited vacancy exists.

### 4. MELBOURNE CBD OFFICE MARKET OUTLOOK

#### 4.1 Demand, Supply and Occupancy

The latest PCA data had shown a 5th period of negative net absorption (-484,400 sq ft). However, vacancy remained stable at 18.0% with 682,430 sq ft of stock withdrawn from the market for refurbishment or conversion, reducing total vacancy. Leasing activity continued to be driven by tenant's relocation to both quality assets and core locations.

#### 4.2 Supply Pipeline

New supply had been moderated over 2024 with only 793,950 sq ft of new or refurbished supply completing, almost half the ten-year annual average. The completion of Melbourne Quarter Tower was the main contributor, bringing 742,700 sq ft of new space to market, 336,000 sq ft of which was committed to Medibank.

<!-- PAGE 62 -->

# Independent Market Report

#### 4.3 Rent and Incentives

Average incentive levels of 48.0%, 22 pps above the pre-pandemic ten-year average, have continued to support tenant demand, although net face rental growth was still being observed. Average prime net face rents increased 4.0% over 2024 led by Premium grade (5.6% y-o-y growth). Average premium grade net incentives were recorded at 44.4%, reflecting a third successive quarterly decline, whereas A-grade net incentives remained higher at 51.6% and are likely to remain elevated along with greater availability of vacant A-grade stock. As a result, prime average net effective rents remained around 7.0% below the September 2022 peak, although Premium grade net effective rents have risen by 6.1% y-o-y compared to A-grade (-2.6%) and B-grade (-7.1%).

#### 4.4 Investment Activity

Over 2024, three assets were transacted totalling A\$600.8 million, a 63.0% increase when compared to 2023 levels, driven by a greater alignment between buyers and vendors. The sale of Mirvac's 367 Collins Street to PAG was the largest transaction with a sale price of A\$315.0 million. The Melbourne CBD average prime yield softened by 0.7pps over the year to 6.5% with minimal further softening expected, aligned to anticipated cash rate cuts over 1H 2025.

#### 4.5 Outlook

Completion of new developments set for late 2025 have been moved into the 2026 pipeline, resulting in no new developments for the year ahead. This should help lower prime vacancy to 16.0% by late 2025, supporting rental growth and slight incentive reductions for well-located Premium and higher quality A-grade assets. Investment activity is expected to rebound as value adjustments ease and debt costs improve.

### 5. ADELAIDE CBD OFFICE MARKET OUTLOOK

#### 5.1 Demand, Supply and Occupancy

Leasing activity in 2024 were driven by tenants relocating and expanding, contributing to significant above historical average net absorption of 556,000 sq ft. A strong performance in 2H 2024 propelled total net absorption to a ten-year high, largely attributable to the ongoing "flight

to quality" trend. Consequently, the Adelaide CBD vacancy rate has declined by 2.9pps to 16.4%, the largest decline in vacancy over 2024 across the nationwide CBD markets.

#### 5.2 Supply Pipeline

Following record levels of new supply entering the market in 2023 (993,500 sq ft), 2024 levels across the Adelaide CBD has been minimal at 143,160 sq ft and mainly attributed to refurbishment activity. As a result, 2024 supply was around half the ten-year annual average.

#### 5.3 Rent and Incentives

Rental growth has been skewed towards prime grade and newer (Gen-3) A-grade, driven by decreasing vacancy and therefore limited options for tenants across prime grade assets. As a result, average prime net face rents grew by 4.7% for Gen-3 assets compared to 3.8% for older A-grade. Average gross prime incentives held steady over 2024 at 26.0% for Gen-3 A-grade and 37.0% for A-grade some downward movement is expected as vacancy continues to taper. Average net effective rents have recorded an increase of 6.3% y-o-y for Gen-3 and 2.6% for A-grade.

#### 5.4 Investment Activity

Three office transactions, each exceeding A\$10 million, have been recorded in 2024, totalling approximately A\$123.81 million. The largest of these was the sale of A-grade Gen 2 asset at 121 King William Street to private investor Kyren Group for approximately A\$84.11 million.

Yields stabilised towards the end of 2024, supported by improved market sentiment with average prime yields currently around 8.0%. The peak-to-trough yield change for this cycle reached 2.1pps for prime assets, resulting in an 18.8% decrease in average capital values.

#### 5.5 Outlook

Adelaide's CBD office market faces continued supply constraints, with only 50 Franklin Street and Market Square currently under construction. 50 Franklin Street is expected to be delivered in 1H 2025, adding approximately 226,000 sq ft of office space with a current pre-commitment rate of 17%. This will likely spur further vacancy reductions and the recovery of effective rental levels over 2025.

## LONDON CENTRAL OFFICE REVIEW

### 1. ECONOMIC OVERVIEW

UK GDP returned to pre-pandemic levels in early 2022, and inflation has slowed after the surge in 2022/2023 following the start of the Ukraine war, with rates reduced to 4.5% in February 2025. The Bank of England forecasts GDP growth of 0.8% in 2025, strengthening to 1.5% in 2026 and 2027.

Despite Brexit, the pandemic and economic volatility, London has maintained its status as a global financial hub. Major companies like BDO, Evercore and Blackstone have established new headquarters in Central London. Office-based employment growth in London has outpaced the rest of the UK, rising by 1.9% annually from 2020 to 2024, with a forecasted 1.3% growth annually from 2025 to 2029.

After a strong start to 2024 with GDP rising by 1.1% in 1H 2024, uncertainty in 2H 2024 caused by an early election and a major shift in fiscal policy weighed on economic performance. GDP for Q3 2024 was flat, while the Q4 2024 growth rate of 0.1% was below expectation. Excluding government consumption and government investment, GDP would have declined. PMI (Purchasing Managers index) figures for January and February suggested that economic growth would remain flat at the start of 2025. According to the PMI report, employment levels fell at the strongest rate since the global financial crisis, possibly

driven by the increase in employer national insurance contributions and increased minimum wage implemented in the beginning of the financial year 2025/2026.

### 2. LONDON OFFICE MARKET OVERVIEW

Annual leasing transactions for 2024 reached 10.8 million sq ft, which was marginally down y-o-y. Take-up was 2.0% down on the ten-year average of 11.0 million sq ft. However, take-up for new or refurbished (1.9 million sq ft) space was up compared to 2023 (1.7 million sq ft). There is currently 11.0 million sq ft of active demand which is 5% above the ten-year average. The demand is attributed mainly to banking and professional services which contributed approximately 6.0 million sq ft.

London office vacancy fell below 10.0% for the first time in 12 months with availability of new or refurbished space at a 12-month low. Total space under construction fell by 5.0% in 2024 due to completed schemes and no pipeline of new supply. Of the 15.9 million sq. ft under construction, 40.0% is pre-let or under offer. 12.0% of the speculative pipeline was absorbed in 2024 with an average transaction size of 77,000 sq ft.

Prime rents across London have grown by 6.6% y-o-y which represents the highest annual uplift in a decade.

While London investment volumes in 2024 was 8.0% down y-o-y at £6.1 billion, transactional activity showed a significant improvement in Q4 2024. Quarterly transaction volume in Q4 2024 at £1.9 billion was the highest since Q1 2023. Greater certainty on pricing, alongside interest rate cuts and an increase in the supply of newly launched buildings for sale, will help to create greater liquidity.

### 3. WEST END OFFICE MARKET

Annual take-up in 2024 for the West End market fell to a four-year low of 3.5 million sq ft. Take-up in 2H 2024 saw an improvement of 68.0% as compared to 1H 2024. Financial and banking services were a key component of demand, accounting for 40.0% of space taken in units over 10,000 sq ft.

While the overall Grade A take-up in 2024 fell by 16.0% to 65.0% of the annual take-up, nine of the ten largest transactions were for Grade A space, totalling 815,000 sq ft or 24.0% of all Grade take-up.

West End annual completions rose to 1.7 million in 2024, of which 55.0% was speculative space. This contributed to an overall rise in vacancy y-o-y from 7.0% to 7.6% in 2024. The availability of new or refurbished space also rose by 13%, reaching the highest level in a decade, while the quarterly take-up of new or refurbished space in Q4 2024 was the highest since Q3 2015.

A total of 5.9 million sq ft currently under construction in the West End is slated for completion between 2025-2027, of which 40% has secured tenants.

West End prime average rent rose to £103.21 per sq ft. Average annual rental growth rose by 7.3% in 2024, with Mayfair, St James's and Soho seeing double digit uplift. The West End prime rent for non-terraced floors now stands at £155.00 per sq ft which represents an annual uplift of 13.6%, from £138.50 per sq. ft in Q4 2023.

The West End market recorded its highest quarterly investment transaction volume of the year in Q4 2024 at £1.26 billion, although overall transaction volumes for 2024 remained at about 50.0% below the ten-year average. The West End prime yield experienced some modest compression in 2024, with the Q4 2024 prime yield at 4.3%.

### 4. OUTLOOK FOR WEST END

Demand picked up at the end of 2024, suggesting a strong start to 2025. Active demand remains healthy with new requirements totalling over 1.5 million sq ft of in the month of December 2024. Pipeline of new supply is likely to secure occupiers who are looking to secure new premises ahead of their lease expiry. Headline rents are expected see further uplift in 2025 with rents for premium and terrace spaces edging up to new record levels.

<!-- PAGE 65 -->

### 5. CITY OFFICE MARKET

The City market saw annual leasing transaction levels rise to a five-year high, reaching 6.3 million sq ft. In Q4 2024, pre-letting activity also reached its highest quarterly level for 12 months (685,000 sq ft). Demand for Grade A offices continues to rise with 75.0% of space transacted in 2024 of Grade A quality.

Average take-up was significantly higher with vacancy falling to its lowest level since 2022. Financial services, banking and professional services (legal and insurance) accounted for 50.0% of total take-up in 2024. All Grade City wide vacancy as at Q4 2024 stands at 10.2%, compared to 12.1% as at 1H 2024. New Grade A vacancy fell to 1.3%, a four-year low. Second-hand availability fell to below 10.0 million sq ft for the first time in 2024 but remains close to 50.0% above the ten-year average.

There was a shortage of speculative space coming to market, with only 269,000 sq ft delivered in 2024 in the City core. However, 2025 will see close to 1.0 million sq ft of speculative space to be completed over the next 12 months. 70% of completions in 2025 are either pre-let or in negotiation for lease. The letting pipeline increased sharply in Q4 2024 with 675,000 sq. ft being leased or in negotiation for lease.

The prime rent for non-tower space in Q4 2024 rose by 14.6% y-o-y to £90.00 per sq ft as compared to £78.50 in Q1 2024. The average rent achieved in Q4 2024 across all lettings for new or pre-let and including premium or tower space stood at £94.00 per sq ft, which was up from £85.00 per sq ft for the whole of 2024.

The City market recorded its highest quarterly investment transaction volume of the year in Q4 at £681 million, which is also the highest since Q3 2023. Total annual volumes remained subdued at 70.0% of the ten-year average level. City prime yield as at Q4 2024 remained at 5.3%.

### 6. OUTLOOK FOR CITY

Active demand remains healthy with major requirements from Bank of America, Lockton and Jane Street accounting for 1.0 million sq ft of new demand and expected to be committed in 1H 2025. The pipeline of new supply will be below trend in 2025 keeping vacancy on a downward trajectory. Occupiers will continue to secure new premises ahead of their lease expiry. Headline rents will see further uplift in 2025 with rents for premium and tower space edging up to new record levels.

## Investor Communications

![A large audience seated in a conference hall, attending a meeting or presentation.](d62e4b7585fe7f7ce5e9beed6b958bd4_img.jpg)

A large audience seated in a conference hall, attending a meeting or presentation.

The Manager is committed towards upholding the utmost standards of accountability to Suntec REIT's unitholders. It achieves this through good corporate transparency practices, maintaining an active channel of communication for investors, analysts and other stakeholders to access accurate and timely information on Suntec REIT, and in working towards fostering good long-term relationships with its stakeholders.

The senior management team of the Manager held regular virtual meetings and conference calls with institutional investors throughout the year. Our participation in various key regional equity and property conferences as well as seminars, enabled us to remain accessible to both institutional and retail investors and gave us the opportunity to provide key strategic and performance updates on Suntec REIT. The fifteenth annual general meeting of Suntec REIT unitholders in April 2024, was held in a wholly physical format and was well-attended by retail investors. It was an opportune time for senior management of the Manager to actively engage retail investors in their enquiries and discussions about Suntec REIT.

The Manager conducts regular business updates and post-results briefings on a quarterly and half-yearly basis respectively. There is extensive coverage on Suntec REIT, with research coverage by analysts from 16 local and foreign brokerage firms, providing a global reach to shareholders and potential investors worldwide. Interim updates are also provided to unitholders in addition to the mandatory financial statements. The Investor Relations department also provides the Board with regular updates on the feedback received from investors.
